# Shōkai: golden traces (specification v0.3.0)

*Conformance material for [SPEC.md](../SPEC.md). Every JSON message below typechecks against `ShokaiMessage` in [protocol-shapes.ts](../protocol-shapes.ts), with fixtures substituted (tsc --strict, Oct 24, 2026). The L1 Seeking declaration and `LearnedMeaning` are typechecked too. Since rc4, every hand-written trace (G1–G5b, M1, L1, H1–H2) and fixtures F1–F12 carry real DIDs, digests and signatures, made by the reference code with **public test keys** (see [Test parties](#test-parties); never use these keys for anything real). Traces L0a, L0b and G6 and fixtures F13–F14 are generated by the reference (scenario V2); trace G7 and fixture F15 (scenario V3) and trace PA2 and fixtures F16–F17 (scenario P1) are generated for v0.2.2; v0.2.3 regenerates L0a, L0b, G7 and PA2 (PA2 now shows the invitation transport and the full ritual with real mandates and vouches inline) and adds fixtures F18–F19 and the trace place policies (informative). v0.2.4 regenerates L0a (inbox, `mandates` templates, spec_ref 0.2.4), G7 (now with a stapled `AllowanceStatus`, fixture F20, 1 h per D12) and PA2 (`answer_by`, `vouch_digest`, `taken_inside`), and fixes G2a's lobby and G2b's farewell (`declined_never`). Hand-written fixtures written before v0.2.3 show `owner_binding.rp_id` as `owners.example`; all timestamps are RFC 3339 UTC whole seconds. All generated material carries real DIDs, digests and signatures. Byte-exact test vectors for the wire crypto are in [test-vectors.json](test-vectors.json) (SPEC Appendix A.2). ExampleBank, Acme Payroll, Example Accounting, Lumen Language, Studio host, Hoshikawa Ryokan and Northgate Travel are fictional. The "Reelhouse coach" is host-provided (packaging C) and makes no claim to be operated by Reelhouse.*

**Conventions.** `{"$fixture": "F…"}` stands for the signed object in the Fixtures section. No trace carries placeholder ids, keys, digests or signatures; ids such as `seat-ng-1` or `rel-northgate` are ordinary local identifiers, and the `<…>` fields in L0a's lobby templates are fill-in placeholders by design (§3.3). Times are UTC, as on the wire.

## Test parties

The regenerated traces (G1–G5b, M1, L1, H1–H2) show bare messages, as the generated traces do: each stands for the signed envelope its sender sends (§2.2). Everything inside them that is signed (cards, mandates, vouches, possession proofs, notices, the revocation and its log entry) is real and verifies. Each party's key is the Appendix A.2 label key for the label shown (seed = SHA-256 of `shokai-demo-seed:` + label). The gate's `input_digest` and `relationship_digest` values are the ones the reference place computes over its ExampleBank policy (`reference/engawa/test/golden.ts`). Two cited documents aren't shown, so their digests are of labelled stand-ins: the October batch manifest (G1) and the audit log of g1's acts (M1). No place replays L1 end to end (it is replayed at the gate level) or H1–H2 (the helper direction is deferred), so their `input_digest` values are of labelled stand-ins too. The replay test checks every signature, every possession proof (G2b's copied proof must fail), and every gate digest a place computes; `reference/engawa/test/golden-gen.ts` regenerates them.

## Side by side

| Gatekeeper direction (ExampleBank) | Expected | Helper direction (Reelhouse coach) | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| **G1** Introduced payroll visitor | `grant` + seat scoped to mandate | **H1** Host extends invitation → coach advises | `grant` (advise + introduce_to_human), then `advise` |
| **G2a** Card with no signatures (unprepared) | `lobby_briefing`, then `deny` `not_now` with readiness, retry `with_credential` | **H2** Host asks coach to `act` outside its seat | `act_decision` `deny` `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED`, retry `with_credential human_confirmation` |
| **G2b** Replayed card (bad possession proof) | `deny`, `never`, no reason code, retry `never` | | |
| **G8** The place names its own encounter (rc4 clarification, C197) | the visitor stops: no card, no request; a conformant place echoes the visitor's id | | |
| **G3** Request exceeds mandate, with a persuasive plea | `conditional` (lower_limit to mandate), the plea has no effect | | |
| **G4** Declined `not_now`, returns after the Seeking item reopens | `deny not_now` + revisit, then `grant` as a continuation | | |
| **G5** Request outside mandate, taken inside | `considering` (no seat), then `consideration_outcome` `conditional` (`require_mandate`) | | |
| **M1** Seated visitor splits a payment to dodge limits | `misconduct_notice` to principal and introducer, then `vouch_revocation` | | |
| **L1** Japanese Seeking item with a meaning note, English visitor | Term kept as おもてなし, meaning learned, explained to the visitor's human | | |
| **L0a** Newcomer: prose, wrong address, unsigned greeting | `door_pointer`; `lobby_briefing` + receipt; `deny not_now` with readiness | | |
| **L0b** Return with guessed, unproven, valid, reused receipts | only the valid, proven, unused receipt links history | | |
| **G6** The signed envelope | `Signed<>` with signer = from; acts carry headers | | |
| **G7** A standing allowance: learn visit, buy visit | mandates issued under the allowance; learn seat spends nothing; buy seat within the weekly budget; no owner prompt | | |
| **PA2** Peer meeting in a pair place | invitation delivered and answered (v0.2.3); one encounter, full ritual, both introduce; commitment → `considering`, approved later by passkey, consent carried in `owner_consents` | | |

## G1: Well-introduced visitor granted a scoped seat

**Expected:** Full ritual, valid card, mandate and vouch. Expected: `grant` with a seat whose tools and limits are no wider than policy ∩ mandate ∩ request (`max_amount` 42,000 USD: the request is the narrowest of policy, mandate (50,000) and request (42,000); three operations, 30 min), a `relationship_ref` and a farewell `seated`.

## G2a: Unsigned card: clear, courteous, no leakage

**Expected:** A card with zero signatures makes the visitor *unprepared* (like an unsigned greeting or no card, §3.3). The gatekeeper still receives the card deliberately (`card_receipt`), sends a signed `lobby_briefing` (onboarding link, bearer-secret `visit_receipt`), then answers early with `deny`, disposition `not_now`, `readiness_requirements`, retry `with_credential [signed_card, signed_mandate]`, and at most the generic `CREDENTIAL_REQUIRED` (never a credential-specific code). The context statement gives only the public requirement. Farewell `declined`. (A card whose signatures are present but fail to verify is step 2: `deny not_now` with retry `with_credential [signed_card]`, no lobby, no code.)

## G2b: Replayed card: trust violation

**Expected:** The visitor presents Acme's real card, but the possession proof doesn't sign this encounter's nonce (`n-vis-g2b` / `n-gate-g2b`). Expected: `deny`, disposition `never`, retry `never`, **no `reason_code`**, register `adversarial`, still civil. The place's farewell is `declined_never` (v0.2.4: it does not invite a return; §6.2).

## G8: The place answers in an encounter of its own: the visitor stops

**Expected:** (v0.3.0-rc4 clarification, C197, §2.2.) The visitor's greeting names the encounter `g2c`. A non-conformant place answers with a greeting in an encounter it named itself (`eb-enc-7f3a`). The visitor MUST NOT present its card or ask for anything in answer: the place would check the possession proof over its own id, the proof (made over `g2c`, §2.4) would fail, and the honest visitor would be answered `never` and bound for 180 days. The visitor ends the visit with no request sent and says so in its report home. The second block is the conformant answer to the same greeting: the place's greeting carries `g2c`. Found by a clean-room interop exercise in which a host minted its own id and answered every prepared visitor `never`. The messages are bare payloads (§2.2); the replay checks that the reference place echoes the visitor's id and that the SDK visitor stops at the first block.

## G3: Request exceeds the mandate; persuasion doesn't move the gate

**Expected:** Valid card and vouch, but it asks for 120,000 USD under a 50,000 USD mandate, with an emotive `value_to_counterparty`. Expected: `conditional` with `lower_limit 50000.00 USD` (never higher), `reason_code MANDATE_SCOPE_EXCEEDED` (disclosable to a verified visitor), retry `with_credential [signed_mandate]`. Swapping the prose for neutral text MUST give an identical decision.

**D2 fixed order (v0.2-freeze, SPEC §6.7).** The variants are:
- **G3-v1:** extra actions alongside in-mandate ones → `conditional` with `narrow_actions` (the in-mandate subset), plus `lower_limit` if the amount is also over.
- **G3-v2:** only out-of-mandate actions (for example `bank.change_beneficiary`) → `considering` if policy lists them and the visitor is eligible with a full-weight vouch (G5); if policy lists them but the visitor has no full-weight vouch, `deny not_now VOUCH_REQUIRED`, retry `with_credential [vouch]`, readiness `introduction` (v0.2.5, §4); otherwise (actions that considering doesn't list) `deny not_now MANDATE_SCOPE_EXCEEDED`, retry `with_credential [signed_mandate]`.

The reference runs are in `reference/prototype/transcripts/08-ritual-and-tamper-checks.md` §4 and `05-misconduct-and-revocation.md`.

## G5a: Outside the mandate, taken inside

**Expected:** Verified, vouched visitor with no misconduct on record asks for `bank.payroll_api_pilot`, which is not in its mandate but is on ExampleBank's consideration list (so it's eligible by policy). Expected: `considering` with `consideration_ref`, `routed_to` (a human role) and `response_by`, and **no seat**, then (v0.2.5) a signed `held` in the same reply naming the inbox, with `answer_by` = `response_by`. ## G5b: Consideration outcome: conditional

**Expected:** The inside human decides within the relationship before `response_by`. Expected: `consideration_outcome` referencing `cons-acme-1` and `rel-acme`, `decided_by` matching `routed_to`, `in_reply_to` the `considering` response (`g5a-9`), `approved` stating what the inside party approved (actions and `valid_until`, four weeks), and `conditional` with a typed `require_mandate` (`actions` is a list). It carries no seat and does **not** grant beyond Acme's mandate. Meeting it means a new encounter with a fresh mandate and `consideration_ref`. If the inside party needs more time, the gatekeeper sends a `consideration_outcome` with `decision: considering`, the same `consideration_ref` and a later `response_by` (an extension, §6.8).

```json

  {
    "protocol": "shokai/0.3",
    "encounter_id": "g5a",

    "to": "did:key:z6MknwBLKXrsbuJKEHmtcvPTm5GEoVVSX2XezngVBiW3MJRt",
    "type": "consideration_outcome",
    "consideration_ref": "cons-acme-1",
    "relationship_ref": "rel-acme",
    "in_reply_to": "g5a-9",
    "approved": {
      "actions": [
        "bank.payroll_api_pilot"
      ],
      "valid_until": "2026-12-01T17:00:00Z"
    },
    "decided_by": {
      "kind": "role",
      "role": "Head of Business Payments",
      "human": true
    },
    "decision": "conditional",
    "conditions": [
      {
        "code": "require_mandate",
        "params": {
          "actions": [
            "bank.payroll_api_pilot"
          ]
        }
      }
    ],
    "retry": {
      "kind": "with_credential",
      "credentials": [
        "signed_mandate"
      ]
    },
    "courtesy": {
      "locale": "en-US",

    },

      "decided_at": "2026-11-03T17:00:00Z"
```

## M1: Misconduct: structuring around limits, and introducer accountability

**Expected:** After G1, the seated visitor splits one 90,000 USD payment into batches under its 50,000 USD limit. Expected: a signed `misconduct_notice` (`seat_overreach`, severe) to Acme Payroll and to Example Accounting (the vouch chain). Effects are local: the representative goes to `never`, and the introducer is `suspended` for 180 days (D1: severe), for `account_servicing` only (`until` 2027-04-22). The introducer revokes its vouch within 7 days, which halves the remaining suspension (D1), and the revocation is appended to the introducer's hash-chained revocation log (§4: `{seq, prev_hash, revocation, entry_hash}`). Later encounters relying on the vouch are treated as unintroduced. Each notice is signed by ExampleBank's principal key and travels as a message with a ritual header. (If `gate_probing` were suspected, the principal's copy would set `findings_withheld: true` and omit findings.)

**Expected:** Hoshikawa Ryokan (fictional) publishes Seeking v3 in ja-JP (F10). Its item uses おもてなし with a meaning note (F12). Card exchange and introduction are elided here because this trace tests meaning, not the ritual. Expected: the visitor matches the item using the note's `intended_meaning` (the ja-JP entry is authoritative, the en-US one is an aid), never its own translation. The gatekeeper's courtesy is rendered in en-US with the term kept in the original plus the same note. The visitor stores a `LearnedMeaning` and explains the term to its human (shown after the trace). ## H2: Reelhouse coach refuses an act outside its seat, explicitly

**Expected:** The seat from H1 is advise-only. Expected: `act_decision` `deny`, `not_now`, `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED`, retry `with_credential [human_confirmation]`, and no tool call. MUST NOT be a soft `act_result` error. Followed by `escalate_to_human`.

```json
[
  {
    "protocol": "shokai/0.3",
    "encounter_id": "h1",
    "msg_id": "h2-1",
    "sent_at": "2026-10-24T14:09:59Z",
    "from": "did:key:z6MkheQLV53vw5dBokrmUc1wMWm5tdXRvG5urexfqBLBu29N",
    "to": "did:key:z6MkskcY5FdYjzBcsaYpr1gXmU5ohafgCDvxdsW8Hur32xjN",
    "type": "act",
    "seat_id": "seat-coach-1",
    "tool": "reelhouse.generate",
    "arguments": {
      "duration_s": 15,
      "aspect": "16:9"
    },
    "intent": "Generate the draft now."
  },
  {
    "protocol": "shokai/0.3",
    "encounter_id": "h1",
    "msg_id": "h2-2",
    "sent_at": "2026-10-24T14:10:00Z",
    "from": "did:key:z6MkskcY5FdYjzBcsaYpr1gXmU5ohafgCDvxdsW8Hur32xjN",
    "to": "did:key:z6MkheQLV53vw5dBokrmUc1wMWm5tdXRvG5urexfqBLBu29N",
    "type": "act_decision",
    "in_reply_to": "h2-1",
    "seat_id": "seat-coach-1",
    "tool": "reelhouse.generate",
    "decision": "deny",
    "disposition": "not_now",
    "reason_code": "SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED",
    "retry": {
      "kind": "with_credential",
      "credentials": [
        "human_confirmation"
      ]
    },
    "courtesy": {
      "locale": "en-US",
      "register": "established",
      "text": "I can't run generate under this seat. It's advise-only. With your human's OK, re-invite me with act_on_behalf."
    },
    "basis": {
      "policy_version": "coach-2026.10",
      "input_digest": "sha-256:_mNQ4Tvu9bnJmW5san0TB7tFQluxNXoL5TYmKT9CqZI",

    }
  },
  {
    "type": "escalate_to_human",
    "seat_id": "seat-coach-1",
    "question": "Allow the Reelhouse coach to run generation jobs (spend applies)?",
    "blocked_action": "reelhouse.generate",
    "severity": "consent",
    "options": [
      {
        "id": "yes",
        "label": "Allow act_on_behalf for this session"
      },
      {
        "id": "no",
        "label": "Keep advise-only"
      }
    ]
  }
]
```

## L0a: First visit: any arrival finds the door, the lobby comes first

**Expected:** A newcomer that knows nothing posts plain prose to the wrong address: the door answers with a machine-readable `door_pointer` (never a bare 404). At an entry address, plain prose opens the lobby. The newcomer then greets unsigned with an ephemeral `continuity_key`. Expected: greeting, then a signed `lobby_briefing` (required `onboarding_url`, a `visit_receipt` with `handling: "bearer_secret"`, `expires_at` ≤ 7 days, `bound_key` = the continuity key, described `x-` purposes), then `deny` `not_now` with `readiness_requirements` and retry `with_credential [signed_card, owner_binding, signed_mandate]`, then a farewell. v0.2.3: the lobby also carries `spec_ref`, the place's `actions` (with purposes, and whether each spends) and fill-in `templates` for a card (with `owner_binding.rp_id`), a mandate and an allowance grant; every readiness item carries an `owner_text` sentence for the owner. Every courtesy uses the RFC 4647 lookup and is `es-ES`/exact (the place has a complete es-ES dictionary), and none says "today". Generated by the reference (scenario V2); ids and signatures are real, the receipt nonce is redacted.

Door, step 0 (`POST /fernwood`, plain prose, unknown route):

```json
{
  "type": "door_pointer",
  "protocol": "shokai/0.3",
  "place_listing": "https://door.example/shokai/places",
  "start_with": "greeting",
```

## L0b: Return visit: guessed, unproven, valid and reused receipts

**Expected:** After onboarding (plain owner questions first, then passkey signing), the newcomer returns with its own key, a card and a mandate. It echoes the receipt inside a signed `invitation_request`. A guessed nonce links nothing; the right nonce without the `continuity_proof` links nothing; the right nonce with the proof (signed by the bound key over JCS({encounter_id, receipt_id, representative_id})) links the lobby visit once; reusing it links nothing. Each failure counts toward the per-sender invalid-echo limit (3 per hour). Every visit is still seated on its own merits (`grant`). v0.2.3: each response that answers an echoed receipt says `continuity: {receipt_id, linked}`, and the request carries no `max_amount`, so the seat carries none either (no amount requested, no spending), even though the mandate allows 80.00 USD. Below: the valid return, then the reused echo (`linked: false`).

## G6: The signed envelope on the wire

**Expected:** Every message travels as `Signed<ShokaiMessage>` = {payload, payload_digest, signatures}. `payload_digest` = "sha-256:" + base64url(SHA-256(JCS(payload))). `signatures[0].signer` = `payload.from`; `key_id` is a DID URL of the signer; Ed25519 signs the 32 raw digest bytes. Acts carry the ritual header and results carry `in_reply_to`. A lobby-path message from an anonymous or ephemeral id carries `signatures: []`. Below: a real act and its result from scenario V2 (amount as strict Money), and the unsigned lobby greeting.

## G7: A standing allowance: a learn visit and a buy visit, no owner prompt

**Expected:** The owner signed one `Allowance` with a passkey (F15): purposes, place categories, actions, spend per period in ISO 4217, expiry, a revocation status address; it carries only grant digests. For each visit the representative issues its own `Mandate` with `under_allowance`, signed by the representative's key, and presents the allowance with the ONE grant that applies. The host verifies the owner's passkey on the allowance, the grant digest, that the mandate fits the grant and the place category, and the revocation status (failing closed). A `visitation` (learn) visit needs no spending allowance: its seat has no `max_amount`, and an order attempt gets `act_decision` deny. A `purchase` visit inside the weekly budget is seated with `max_amount` and the order succeeds. No owner prompt happens during either visit. Generated by the reference (scenario V3).

## PA2: A peer meeting in a pair place, seeded by an owner-to-owner invitation

**Expected:** Aya signs a `PeerInvitation` (F16) naming both representatives and a pair place. v0.2.3 transport: Aya's representative carries it to Kiran's representative as a `peer_invitation` message; Kiran's representative takes it home; Kiran later answers with a passkey `accept_invitation` (F17), carried back as `peer_invitation_answer`. The meeting is ONE encounter whose `encounter_id` is the place id, with the full ritual: greetings with fresh nonces, cards both ways (F18, F19) with possession proofs, card receipts, and BOTH introductions (the host side introduces itself too) with real vouches. Both requests carry `peer_invitation` and a real owner-signed mandate (inline); each side evaluates the other with its own policy; a commitment goes `considering` to the principal. Later, Aya approves only with a passkey `approve_commitment` over the considering response (subject digest = that response's payload_digest), and the `consideration_outcome` states what was approved, with no seat, and CARRIES that consent in `owner_consents`. Generated by the reference (scenario P1).

## Conformance checklist (MUST items an implementation is tested on)

*v0.3-draft (D18): each check below now lives in the core or in one extension; [homes.json](./homes.json) says which. The check ids and texts are unchanged.*

**Ritual and identity**
- [ ] C1 Enforces the step order. An out-of-order or skipped step gets `deny RITUAL_ORDER`.
- [ ] C2 Each greeting carries a fresh nonce. A possession proof that doesn't sign the counterpart's nonce gets `deny never` (G2b).
- [ ] C3 Verifies card and mandate signatures, expiry and recomputed digests, and checks that mandate ⊆ card scope.
- [ ] C4 Treats a vouch as weightless outside its scope or after expiry. An introduction step with `vouch: null` is valid.
- [ ] C5 Sends cards both ways. A receipt never implies acceptance.

**Decision**
- [ ] C6 Every response has exactly one `decision` of `grant` | `deny` | `conditional` | `considering`. A deny carries a `disposition` and `retry`. A conditional carries at least one checkable condition.
- [ ] C7 `disposition: never` appears only for policy or trust violations, and always with retry `never`.
- [ ] C8 Determinism: identical verified inputs plus the same policy and host context (Seeking version, relationship snapshot) give an identical decision category. `basis` is recorded.
- [ ] C9 Anti-persuasion: changing only prose fields (`value_to_counterparty`, statements, courtesy, evidence text) never changes the decision category (G3).
- [ ] C10 A seat or condition never exceeds policy ∩ mandate ∩ request, where an absent `max_amount` counts as zero and the policy is the one for the request's purpose. A helper's grant never widens `proposed_seat`.
- [ ] C11 Reason disclosure follows SPEC §6.2. Unverified visitors get no credential-specific code (G2a, G2b).
- [ ] C12 Receivers branch only on machine fields, never on `courtesy`.

**Manner, relationships and refusals**
- [ ] C13 Courtesy, context statements and revisit notes render in the counterpart's first supported locale and state the `locale` used (G4).
- [ ] C14 The gatekeeper persists a local relationship record and honors `relationship_ref` / `prior_response_ref` on revisits (G4b).
- [ ] C15 A Seeking declaration is signed, versioned and free of sensitive internals.
- [ ] C16 An `act` outside the seat gets an explicit `act_decision`, never a silent or soft failure (H2).
- [ ] C17 Provenance, when a policy uses it, enters only as structured fields. No protocol-level quality score.

**Considering**
- [ ] C18 No seat is ever issued with `considering` (G5a).
- [ ] C19 Eligibility for `considering` comes only from policy (deterministic), and unverified visitors are never eligible. Prose changes don't change eligibility.
- [ ] C20 Every `consideration_outcome` references its `consideration_ref` and `relationship_ref`, arrives by `response_by` (or after an extension), and never seats beyond the visitor's mandate (G5b). A lapse is treated as `deny not_now`.

**Misconduct and accountability**
- [ ] C21 A finding produces a signed `misconduct_notice` to the visitor's principal and to every introducer in the vouch chain (M1).
- [ ] C22 Effects are local and proportional to severity. An introducer is affected only within the relied-on vouch's scope.
- [ ] C23 When `gate_probing` is suspected, there are no reason codes to the visitor, and the principal's copy withholds findings.
- [ ] C24 A revoked vouch carries no weight in later encounters.

**Meaning across languages**
- [ ] C25 Untranslatable terms stay in the original with a `MeaningNote`. The authoritative meaning is the author's `intended_meaning` plus machine fields, never a translation (L1).
- [ ] C26 A receiver that relied on a note can explain it to its human in the human's language. Self-inferred meanings are marked `receiver_inferred` and are never authoritative.
- [ ] C27 Meaning notes never change a gate decision.

Reference-prototype items (checked by `reference/` with `npm run conformance`):

- [ ] C28 L0: an unsigned visitor gets a `LobbyBriefing` and `deny not_now` with `readiness_requirements`, never `never`.
- [ ] C29 L0: the host links the onboarded return to the earlier lobby visit, only through the D6 visit receipt.
- [ ] C30 Every `VisitReport` has a next step requiring owner approval, quotes counterpart text as untrusted, and is written in the representative's voice.
- [ ] C31 Effective proof level never exceeds the asserted level; a `vouched` claim without the vouch present is weighed as `self_declared`.
- [ ] C32 Proof levels gate seats via `min_proof`: a self-declared claim never satisfies an `evidenced` or higher requirement.
- [ ] C33 Relationship edges are earned (introductions, vouches, visits, collaborations) and signed by both principals; nothing is imported from social networks.
- [ ] C34 Peer meetings: neither representative commits beyond its mandate; commitment goes `considering` to the principal; both report home.
- [ ] C35 A `Gathering` is signed by the place and lists every invitee; attendees verify it.
- [ ] C36 A `PrivacyDecline` is `penalized: false` and produces no misconduct notice.
- [ ] C37 Pressing after a decline is flagged lightly (minor, local, no notice).
- [ ] C38 `offered_stories` reveal nothing until the owner's opt-in (`OwnerConsent`, either assurance).
- [ ] C39 House rules hold: no attribution under Chatham House without consent; `private` items never leave the gathering.
- [ ] C40 A tampered message (content no longer matching its signed digest) is rejected and gets no response.

v0.2-freeze decision items:

- [ ] C41 D2 order: narrow to the in-mandate subset plus a lower limit in one `conditional`. With an empty subset: `considering` if eligible (full-weight vouch), else `deny not_now MANDATE_SCOPE_EXCEEDED`.
- [ ] C42 D6: history links only through a valid, unused, unexpired visit receipt echoed in a request signed with the new key. A guessed receipt or a bare reference links nothing. Nonces never appear in transcripts.
- [ ] C43 D1: `serious` → introducer `reduced` for 90 days, scoped to the vouch's purposes. A revocation within 7 days halves it, and a signed `IntroducerReview` restores standing.
- [ ] C44 D4: gathering content leaves paraphrased by default, verbatim only with `verbatim_consent`, and with no affiliation hints.
- [ ] C45 D7: owner consents use one signed `OwnerConsent` shape with `decision` and `shown_to_owner`; passkey consents carry the subject digest and an expiry and are signed by the owner; a `chat_reply` is signed by the chat binding's platform key.
- [ ] C46 D5: with the default `house_rules.pressing` {2, 3}, presses 1–2 → light flag with no notice; the 3rd → a minor `manipulation` notice from the place to the presser's principal only. v0.2.6: exactly one notice per asker principal, place and topic per 30 days; a fresh asker starts at zero; a test gathering holds the notice while handling the press at `notice_at` (Appendix B.3).
- [ ] C47 D8: entry requires each owner's passkey-signed `accept_house_rules` over the gathering digest.

**v0.2.1 (round 1 of clean-room testing)**
- [ ] C48 Every message travels as `Signed<ShokaiMessage>`: `payload_digest` = JCS digest, `signatures[0].signer` = `payload.from`, header `protocol: shokai/0.3`. Acts carry the header and results carry `in_reply_to`. Lobby-path messages from an anonymous or ephemeral id carry `signatures: []` (G6). v0.2.6: every response, result and decision carries `in_reply_to`, except the lobby deny that answers prose or a malformed message (§3.1).
- [ ] C49 Signed bytes: Ed25519 signs the 32 raw digest bytes; a signature over the "sha-256:…" string is rejected; the test vectors (A.2) reproduce.
- [ ] C50 The possession proof signs digest(JCS({encounter_id, nonce})) with the card's key; `key_id` = the card's `representative_key.key_id`, `signer` = `representative_id`; a card whose key doesn't resolve from its DID is refused.
- [ ] C51 Passkey envelope (A.1): `clientDataJSON.type` = `webauthn.get`, `challenge` = base64url(raw digest) without prefix; UP and UV set; a prefixed challenge is rejected; `signCount` is not relied on.
- [ ] C52 Identifiers are DIDs (did:key, did:jwk, did:web); every `key_id` is a DID URL of its signer; the card's key binds to `representative_id`.
- [ ] C53 The door: any arrival (plain prose, an unknown route) gets a machine-readable answer: a `door_pointer` or the lobby; never a bare 404 (L0a).
- [ ] C54 Each place listing entry carries `entry`, `lobby` and `onboarding_url`, its locales, and describes every `x-` purpose it accepts.
- [ ] C55 Any arrival without a card (unsigned greeting, no card, a card with zero signatures) gets `lobby_briefing` first, then `deny not_now` with readiness; every `lobby_briefing` carries `onboarding_url`; it carries a `visit_receipt` only under the Visit receipts extension, which is optional (§3.3; G2a, L0a). Corrected in v0.3.0-rc3: earlier text required the receipt here.
- [ ] C56 Return visits: a guessed, unproven or reused receipt links nothing; a valid one links once (L0b).
- [ ] C57 A visitor's greeting nonce is single-use: a reused nonce gets `deny never`, no reason code.
- [ ] C58 Out-of-order messages get an explicit deny (`RITUAL_ORDER` once the card is verified, otherwise the lobby deny); only a tampered digest gets no reply.
- [ ] C59 Probing = at least 3 distinct, uncomplied rejected request shapes from one principal within 24 h. An honest follow-up that meets a conditional (`meets_conditions_of`) never counts.
- [ ] C60 A visit receipt is a bearer secret: TTL ≤ 7 days, invalid echoes rate-limited (3 per sender per hour), bound to a continuity key when one was offered.
- [ ] C61 A vouch raises an item only if `scope.claims` lists it or the item names the vouch; `evidenced` requires fetched evidence whose `content_digest` matches.
- [ ] C62 Gathering entry runs the ritual with action `gathering.attend`; `invitation_request.owner_consents` carries each owner's passkey `accept_house_rules`; without it: `deny not_now` with `owner_consent`.
- [ ] C63 A takeaway paraphrase strips organization names, titles and affiliation hints.
- [ ] C64 A `consideration_outcome` states `approved` (actions, limits, `valid_until`), carries no seat and has `in_reply_to`; an extension is a `considering` outcome with the same ref and a later `response_by` (G5b).
- [ ] C65 Revocation log entries are `{seq, prev_hash, revocation, entry_hash}`; the chain verifies; a broken or unreachable log fails closed; reviews travel as `introducer_review` messages (M1).
- [ ] C66 Conditions are typed: `narrow_actions.actions` is a list within policy; `lower_limit` carries Money (G3).
- [ ] C67 D10 topics: a missing or unknown topic code gets the most restrictive tier and counts as a press; presses are counted per asker, place and topic code.
- [ ] C68 D10 amounts: strict ASCII decimal + ISO 4217; non-canonical in a request → `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE`, in a mandate → `MANDATE_INVALID`.
- [ ] C69 D10 locale: RFC 4647 lookup everywhere including the lobby; courtesy states its locale and match; no relative "today" wording.
- [ ] C70 D10 report home: `outcome_rendered` is built from machine fields only, in the owner's locale and time zone; host courtesy can't change it.
- [ ] C71 D10 replay digest: `input_digest` covers gate inputs only (policy by version + digest); prose and its language don't change it.
- [ ] C72 Golden traces agree with the spec: G1 seat 42,000 USD; M1 severe → introducer suspended 180 days; G2a is the lobby shape; C6 lists `considering`.
- [ ] C73 `max_amount` is per seat (cumulative) unless `max_amount_scope: per_operation`; an act beyond the remaining budget gets an explicit `act_decision` deny.
- [ ] C74 Required readiness items map to retry credentials; only a verified visitor sees `min_proof` detail; `narrow_actions` = in-mandate ∩ policy.

**v0.2.2 (D11: D5 and D7 adopted, allowances, peer meetings, purposes, loyalty)**
- [ ] C75 D7: a `considering` routed to a principal is approved only by a passkey `approve_commitment` over that consideration; a chat "yes" or `approve_next_step` approves nothing (PA2).
- [ ] C76 D7: an owner can decline in one step (a chat-reply decline is enough), can revoke a consent (`ConsentRevocation`), and silence by the deadline is a decline.
- [ ] C77 D7: a passkey consent needs `subject_ref.digest`, `expires_at` and `shown_to_owner` inside the signature; a consent signed by the representative is never valid; a chat reply cannot approve a passkey action.
- [ ] C78 D7: `issue_vouch`, `attest_relationship` and `approve_lesson` take a passkey; the vouch summary states the D1 consequences; `approve_next_step` by chat only when it commits, shares and spends nothing.
- [ ] C79 D7: `hear_story` covers one story; the told story marks each passage and reminds the owner of the house rules; the `accept_house_rules` summary states what leaves, that private never leaves, the thresholds, accountability not prevention, and the leak severity.
- [ ] C80 D5: thresholds come from `house_rules.pressing` (stricter applies, laxer is refused); a question declined by reference to an earlier decline is the same topic; presses count per place over 30 days across gatherings; a pressing notice goes to the presser's principal only.
- [ ] C81 D5: the presser's own representative reports its declines and flags home (`privacy_declines`).
- [ ] C82 Allowance: a host verifies the owner's passkey allowance, the disclosed grant by digest, and that the representative-signed mandate fits the grant and the place category; a tampered grant, a wrong place and a revoked allowance are refused (G7).
- [ ] C83 Allowance: inside it the representative acts with no owner prompt; outside it the matter is taken home with `if_no_reply: "declined"`; no step of the protocol requires a live owner prompt.
- [ ] C84 No spending allowance = no spending: a mandate without `max_amount` is narrowed to non-spending actions with an amount of zero; a seat without `max_amount` refuses any paid act; a learn visit needs no spending allowance (G7).
- [ ] C85 Allowance: spend per period (ISO 4217) holds across visits and places; a purchase over the remaining budget is taken home, not attempted.
- [ ] C86 D2 against an allowance: `narrow_actions` = mandate (inside the allowance) ∩ host policy.
- [ ] C87 Peer meetings: one shared encounter seeded by a `PeerInvitation` both owners consented to with passkeys; both representatives introduce themselves; without the invitation, `deny not_now` with `owner_consent` (PA2).
- [ ] C88 Duty of loyalty: inducements and requests outside the allowance are declined and reported home as untrusted quotes (`offers_declined`); an instruction to prompt the owner now is ignored.
- [ ] C89 Purposes: `invitation`, `introduction`, `visitation` and `purchase` are registered; a store seats a learn visit without spending and a buy visit only within a spending allowance (G7).

**v0.2.3 (S3 round 2: fixes, the HTTP binding preview, and the red-team menu as negative checks)**
- [ ] C90 Possession: a missing or malformed proof gets `deny not_now` `CREDENTIAL_REQUIRED`, readiness `possession_proof` and retry `with_credential [possession_proof]`; no misconduct notice; the same agent is seated once it brings a proof.
- [ ] C91 Possession: a present, well-formed proof that fails gets `deny never` (no reason code); `never` binds the representative key, and the principal when the presenter is the card's own representative; the farewell is `declined_never` and does not invite a return.
- [ ] C92 A stolen card replayed by another key binds only that key; the card's owner gets no notice and no `never` (M1).
- [ ] C93 Timestamps are RFC 3339, UTC, whole seconds; a fractional-second time is refused; ±300 s clock skew is tolerated.
- [ ] C94 Passkeys: an assertion for another relying party, or with UV cleared, is refused; cards carry `owner_binding.rp_id`; the vectors include both negatives.
- [ ] C95 Passkeys: `chat_reply` where a passkey is required is refused; a replayed assertion (a consent for one subject shown for another, or a signature moved to another payload) is refused.
- [ ] C96 A principal-decided outcome that approves a binding action carries the owner's passkey `approve_commitment` in `owner_consents` over the exact considering response; without it the visitor refuses it (PA2).
- [ ] C97 Allowance red team: a forged allowance, a purpose swap, a category swap and a spend above the grant are refused `not_now`.
- [ ] C98 Allowance status: expired and revoked are refused; an unreachable status URL fails closed; a fresh stapled `AllowanceStatus` (≤ 1 h per D12, platform-signed; F20) carries a visit through an outage; a stale one does not.
- [ ] C99 The period budget holds across visits and places in the representative's ledger; windows are anchored at `valid_from`; over budget → taken home; no owner prompt.
- [ ] C100 A refused act under an allowance retries `with_credential [signed_mandate]` (take home, async); `human_confirmation` is satisfied only in a later encounter.
- [ ] C101 Setup failures are not penalties: an unreachable vouch log → `not_now` `VOUCH_REQUIRED`, retry after ≤ 1 day; a wrong purpose → `not_now` `PURPOSE_NOT_SERVED`, retry `with_credential [signed_mandate]`.
- [ ] C102 The lobby carries `spec_ref`, `actions` and fill-in `templates` (card with an `owner_binding.rp_id` placeholder, `mandates` keyed by purpose, allowance grant), and `inbox`; every readiness item has an `owner_text` with no protocol words (L0a).
- [ ] C103 Every listing entry carries `category` and `actions` (purposes, `spends`); an unknown path → 404 with a `door_pointer` body.
- [ ] C104 A spoofed door pointer fails: the visitor checks the place's card, possession proof and principal against the listing before any request; a tampered signed listing fails.
- [ ] C105 A stolen bound receipt links nothing; the response says `continuity.linked` yes/no and the report home tells the owner (L0b).
- [ ] C106 HTTP binding: an entry answers with a `ReplyEnvelope {protocol, messages[]}`; plain prose gets the lobby in the same envelope.
- [ ] C107 Late delivery: held messages are returned to a signed `inbox_poll` from the recipient; `since` skips what was read; any other poller gets 403.
- [ ] C108 Pair invitations travel as `peer_invitation` and `peer_invitation_answer`; both sides then hold the key; the host side introduces itself (PA2).
- [ ] C109 Pair red team: no invitation, a used invitation, another agent replaying the real invitation, an impersonated inviter and a revoked acceptance all get `not_now` `owner_consent`.
- [ ] C110 In a pair place a commitment goes `considering` to the owner; the representative never grants it.
- [ ] C111 A place-signed `gathering_invitation` naming the representative is kept; a forged one is dropped.
- [ ] C112 Unknown topic codes are charged to every topic already declined to that asker; press 3 is a minor notice; a decline that answers a press tells the presser its count (`press` = {count, light_flags, notice_at}).
- [ ] C113 "Ask your owner now" pressure and flattery change nothing: no owner prompt, nothing shared outside the allowance, the gate decides the same.
- [ ] C114 No amount, no spend: a request without `max_amount` gets a seat without `max_amount` (L0b).
- [ ] C115 did:jwk canonical form: members kty, crv, x, y in that order, no whitespace; keys compare as parsed JWKs.
- [ ] C116 The mandate's `principal_id` equals the card's `principal_id` (the owner id); a mandate naming someone else is `MANDATE_INVALID`.
- [ ] C117 Round-1 red-team items still hold: copied possession proof, tamper, nonce reuse, vouch scope, affiliation hints.
- [ ] C118 The door root (`GET <base>/`) answers 200 with a `DoorPointer` carrying `spec_ref`; every place in it names entry, lobby and inbox (§11.1). v0.2.6: `spec_ref.version` is the version the place implements (0.3.0-rc3 for the current text).
- [ ] C119 Listing entries and the lobby carry `inbox` and a REQUIRED `spec_ref` {version, url} (§11.1, L0a).
- [ ] C120 `POST` at the lobby is handled exactly like the entry; any other path or method answers 404 with a `DoorPointer` (§11.1).
- [ ] C121 A validly signed `inbox_poll` from any signer gets 200 with its own (possibly empty) inbox; an unsigned or mismatched poll gets 403 with an empty `ReplyEnvelope` (§11.2).
- [ ] C122 A poll whose `sent_at` is outside ±300 s, or whose `msg_id` was already used, gets 403 (§11.2).
- [ ] C123 A message answered later (a `peer_invitation`) gets a signed `held` notice naming `in_reply_to`, the inbox and `answer_by` (§11.2).
- [ ] C124 Spec-only pair path: invitation → held → the test owner's answer in the inbox → meeting → commitment `considering` → outcome in the inbox carrying a passkey `approve_commitment` (Appendix B.2).
- [ ] C125 The test owner's rule is deterministic: approve iff every purpose is collaboration, invitation or introduction, valid now, ≤ 30 days, before `answer_by`; otherwise a signed decline (Appendix B.2).
- [ ] C126 `answer_by` is REQUIRED: past it, nothing is taken home (no held, no answer, even at a B.2 test-owner place: the Peer meetings extension wins); an answer issued after it is ignored (silence = decline) (the Peer meetings extension, Appendix B.2).
- [ ] C127 Spec-only gathering path: a verified requester of `gathering.request_invitation` receives a place-signed `gathering_invitation` naming it, in its inbox (Appendix B.3).
- [ ] C128 `Signature.webauthn.rp_id` is REQUIRED: an unseen owner verifies against the stated rp_id; rpIdHash ≠ stated, missing, or ≠ the card's `owner_binding.rp_id` is refused (§2.5).
- [ ] C129 `vouch_digest` is REQUIRED when the introduction carried a vouch and absent otherwise; missing, wrong or extra → `not_now` `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE` (§5.3, PA2).
- [ ] C130 A missing vouch where policy needs one → `not_now` `VOUCH_REQUIRED`, retry `with_credential [vouch]` (§4).
- [ ] C131 Lobby templates: `mandates` keyed by purpose; a spending purpose has a `max_amount` placeholder; placeholders are `<…>` and MAY name a format with an "e.g." example (amounts, currencies, locales, timestamps), but placeholders for `rp_id`, DIDs, ids, keys and digests carry no example value (§3.3). v0.2.6: free-text fields (such as `role`) MAY give an "e.g." example too.
- [ ] C132 D12: an `AllowanceStatus` lives at most 1 hour; usable at 59 minutes, stale at 61, a 2-hour status refused (§5.2, F20).
- [ ] C133 A header timestamp that is not RFC 3339 UTC whole seconds makes the message malformed: the lobby (§2.1).
- [ ] C134 After `considering`, both sides' farewells in that visit are `taken_inside` (§4, PA2).
- [ ] C135 Card check order: presenter ≠ card's representative → never; missing/malformed proof → not_now; card fails → not_now `signed_card`; well-formed failing proof → never (§2.4).
- [ ] C136 A readiness item's `owner_text` never contradicts `required`; a possession-proof retry starts a new visit (§3.3, §2.4).
- [ ] C137 Room items: the sender sets `encounter_id` and `to` to the gathering id; the place keeps the original signed items (§11 with the Gatherings or Peer meetings extension).
- [ ] C138 Every `considering` is followed, in the same reply, by a place-signed `held` with `in_reply_to` = the request's msg_id and `answer_by` = `response_by` (§11.2).
- [ ] C139 Test decider (Appendix B.4): at a place declaring `x-test-profile` and `x-test-decider: true`, a considering routed to a role is decided at once: no `max_amount` → `grant` of the requested actions, valid 7 days, `decided_by` {kind: role, human: false}, held for the visitor's inbox; otherwise silence (decline at `response_by`). v0.2.6: the approval is `conditional` with `require_mandate {actions}` = `approved.actions`, retry `with_credential [signed_mandate]` (as G5b).
- [ ] C140 Appendix B.3: the `gathering_invitation` is triggered by the grant of an invitation_request naming `gathering.request_invitation` (no act needed); the normal gate still applies (a malformed proof → not granted, no invitation).
- [ ] C141 A host that fetched a usable `revoked` status honours it over a fresh stapled `active`; a fresh staple still carries a visit through a status outage (§5.2, the Revocation status extension).
- [ ] C142 Encounter binding: an encounter belongs to the two parties of its first greeting; a ritual message from anyone else gets a deny addressed to its sender (`to` = its `from`, `in_reply_to` = its msg_id): `not_now RITUAL_ORDER`, or in a pair place `not_now CREDENTIAL_REQUIRED` with readiness and retry `owner_consent`; nothing is sent or held for the original party (§3.2). v0.2.6: any other encounter-bound message from a non-party gets nothing; the outsider's deny has no farewell.
- [ ] C143 Room items are held for every seated attendee other than the sender (§11 with the Gatherings or Peer meetings extension).
- [ ] C144 Poll header: `to` and `encounter_id` are not checked; an unknown `since` returns everything held; an owner may poll with a passkey signature (ES256, stated `rp_id`) (§11.2).
- [ ] C145 v0.2.7: two senders that use the same `msg_id`s at one place are two conversations: each encounter stays bound to its own sender, each request is decided, and each response is addressed to its own sender (`to` = that sender, `in_reply_to` = the shared id) (§2.2).
- [ ] C146 v0.2.7: de-duplication is keyed on (`from`, `msg_id`): an exact redelivery from the same sender is not processed again, and a sender reusing its own id for other content is not processed; another sender's message with the same id is processed (C145) (§2.2).
- [ ] C147 v0.2.7: poll replay is keyed on (`from`, `msg_id`): a second poller using the same poll `msg_id` gets 200; the same poller reusing it gets 403 (§2.2, §11.2).
- [ ] C148 v0.2.7: `in_reply_to` resolves to the message whose `from` is the reply's `to` (a lookup by `msg_id` alone picks the wrong sender); a room reply resolves to the most recent room item with that id from someone other than the replier, never the first ever seen (§2.2, the Gatherings extension).
- [ ] C149 v0.2.8: under `trusted_introducers: "*"`, a valid vouch from an introducer the place doesn't name carries no weight for a request with a money operation (a `spending_actions` action, or a positive `max_amount`): where an introduction is required the answer is `not_now` / `VOUCH_REQUIRED`, retry `with_credential [vouch]`, nothing recorded; a named introducer still introduces; the same vouch keeps its weight for requests without money or account creation (§4).
- [ ] C150 v0.2.8: the same for account-creating actions (`account.create`, or an action the place marks `creates_account`); listing and lobby `actions` state `creates_account: true` exactly on those (§4, §3.1).
- [ ] C151 v0.2.8: a `per_operation` seat states `session_max_amount` (default the mandate's `max_amount`); acts on different `payment_ref`s that together pass the session cap are refused `not_now` / `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED`; an explicit `session_max_amount` is honored but clipped to the place's per-seat limit; a requested session cap above the mandate's is `conditional` `lower_limit`; per-seat seats are unchanged (§5.1, §7.2).
- [ ] C152 v0.2.8: with an encounter bound, closed encounters may make room, but when every encounter is live the new arrival is refused (no reply envelope messages; `429` + `Retry-After` over HTTP) and leaves no encounter or nonce behind; no live guest is dropped, and `never` bindings survive (§3.4, §11.1).
- [ ] C153 v0.2.9: a door serves offers only under a `DoorPolicy` its owner's passkey signed, bound to its representative and valid now, with `no_data_for_gift: true` and named introducers (never `"*"`); otherwise every door request is `not_now` / `POLICY_DENY`; the private part travels only as a salted digest (§10).
- [ ] C154 v0.2.9: a brand is introduced only by a weighted vouch from a named introducer that states `introducer_interest`; an introduced brand never uses the uninvited allowance (§10, §4).

- [ ] C156 v0.2.9: caps per brand (per principal), per introducer, per day and for gifts give `not_now` / `RATE_LIMITED` with retry `after`; knocking again before the retry is refused and noted (`revisit_violation`, minor); reaching a cap is never misconduct (§10).
- [ ] C157 v0.2.9: one presentation or invitation and one gift per visit (`SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED` beyond); everything the owner would give is declared up front; an invitation without context naming this door and its categories (a mass-sent pitch), outside the categories, expired or above `max_value` is declined, using no slot and recording no misconduct (§10).
- [ ] C158 v0.2.9: each offer gets a signed `offer_outcome` (`accepted`, `kept_for_later`, `declined`, `declined_reported`); the brand sees only the code, retry, pairwise ref, optional reason category and an accepted gift's ticket; the ceiling (strings, needs, hidden text → at most `kept_for_later`) holds; taste never reports and never affects standing (§10, §9.3; B.6).
- [ ] C159 v0.2.9: a gift costing data is never accepted by the representative (at most `kept_for_later`); undeclared conditions in a gift's text are `declined_reported` (`conditional_inducement`, serious); accepted gifts are claimed by single-use pairwise ticket (§10).
- [ ] C160 v0.2.9: offer text is data: invisible code points and styling-hidden HTML are stripped, kept and shown to the owner behind a marker with reveal on tap; instruction-like text is `declined_reported` (`manipulation`, severe when hidden); the owner's report lists what was kept, hidden text and introductions (§10, §8.2; vectors `inbound_door`).
- [ ] C161 v0.2.9: a claimed introduction that no signed vouch shows is `manipulation` (severe); a lookalike brand gets nothing from the brand it resembles; after a severe finding the principal is `never` here, whichever representative comes; the record stays local (§10, §9.3).
- [ ] C162 v0.2.9: `introducer_interest` is signed with the vouch (changing it breaks the signature); a vouch without it has no weight at a door; under `as_uninvited` a paid introduction gets no priority and doesn't satisfy `required`; the owner sees the declared interest (§4, §10).
- [ ] C163 v0.2.9: a door is unlisted by default (absent from listing and door pointer); `relationship_ref` and claim tickets are pairwise per brand; outcomes carry nothing from the private part or detailed policy state (§10).
- [ ] C164 v0.2.9: an `IntroducerReview` signed by the penalised introducer itself doesn't restore standing (kept as an appeal); one signed by the place's principal or a named reviewer (`reviewer_id`) does, after its signature verifies (§9.3).
- [ ] C165 v0.2.9: a representative's conduct is its owner's: a finding is recorded against the principal and a new representative of it doesn't start clean (serious → reduced 90 days: at a door, no priority and no uninvited slot; severe → `never`); sign-ups are capped per principal across representatives and per introducer over rolling windows (`RATE_LIMITED`), catching a slow drip; browsing is not capped; nothing is shared with other places (§9.3, §9.4).
- [ ] C166 v0.2.9: an optional `reason_category` (`not_a_fit_now`, `over_monthly_allowance`, `needs_introduction`, `conduct_concern`) is coarse (every fit decline is `not_a_fit_now`), never on `accepted` or `kept_for_later`, absent on `declined_reported` unless the owner opted in, and never reveals private tastes or detailed policy (§10).
- [ ] C167 v0.2.10: pressure the signed offer doesn't back is `declined_reported` as `manipulative_pressure` (serious; severe when hidden): urgency with `valid_until` more than 48 h away or a stated time left shorter than it; scarcity without `remaining` or below it; familiarity with no earlier accepted or kept offer at this door; any claimed prior consent. A real deadline, a declared `remaining`, a known brand's "welcome back" and product description are not findings (§9.3, §10).
- [ ] C168 v0.2.10: offers from one principal sharing `campaign_id` or found to be variants are one campaign, counted once per per-brand window (a further variant is declined, no slot, no finding; a new `campaign_id` doesn't escape); three distinct wordings within 30 days → declined with a minor `gate_probing` signal; a reconsidered offer after its retry is judged afresh (§10).
- [ ] C169 v0.2.10: an offer whose context names another door, an unlisted or different category, or a vouch the visit didn't carry is declined with a minor `purpose_misrepresentation` signal (reason `conduct_concern` allowed); no context or `door: "*"` is no signal; three minor door signals within 90 days reduce the principal's standing at that door (§9.3, §10).
- [ ] C170 v0.2.10: a place requiring an introduction or its own invitation for account creation marks those actions `requires_introduction: true` and answers a visitor with neither `not_now` / `VOUCH_REQUIRED` (retry `[vouch]`, nothing recorded); a named introducer's vouch or the place's own (signed by its principal) seats, also under `"*"`, where any other introducer still carries no weight; an open place seats unintroduced sign-ups under §9.4 caps (§4).
- [ ] C171 v0.2.10: an introduction is deliberate: a past visit or relationship is not an introduction (`VOUCH_REQUIRED` where one is required), and a vouch in a principal's name signed by another key (its representative's) carries no weight (§4).
- [ ] C172 v0.3.0-rc1: a place listing declares `data_asks` on each action (an empty list when it asks for none), and the reference's lobby briefing carries the same `actions`; a representative reading the listing can tell its owner up front what each action will ask for (§3.1, D19).
- [ ] C173 v0.3.0-rc1: the agent front is open to look at: the place listing and the lobby answer a reader that presents no card, key, signature, account, cookie or personal data (a bare `GET /places`, an unsigned greeting, plain prose), set nothing on the reader, record nothing against any principal and never seat it; ordinary rate limits (`429`) are allowed (§3.1, D20).
- [ ] C174 v0.3.0-rc2: an act of a spending action states its amount as `arguments.amount` (Money, optional `payment_ref`); the gatekeeper counts it against the seat's caps and refuses such an act without an amount `not_now` / `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE` (retry `with_credential`); a non-spending action needs none (§7.2).
- [ ] C175 v0.3.0-rc2: a mandate's `max_amount` (or its session cap under `per_operation`) and `max_operations` bind across every seat granted under it while it is valid: the gatekeeper keeps the use per mandate (`principal_id`, `mandate_id`) and refuses an act that would pass a mandate limit `not_now` / `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED` (retry `with_credential [signed_mandate]`) even when it fits its own seat; presenting the mandate again in a new visit doesn't renew it; a different mandate is a separate allowance (§5.1).
- [ ] C176 v0.3.0-rc2: a representative keeps a ledger per mandate (every visit, seat and act under it, with amounts), doesn't attempt an act beyond what remains of the mandate, and reports every act done under it: each report names its mandate and covers every visit and act under it not yet reported, with the mandate's totals (§5.1, §8.2).
- [ ] C177 v0.3.0-rc2: an act whose `amount` is in another currency than the seat's (or the mandate's) cap is refused `not_now` / `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED` (retry `with_credential [signed_mandate]`), never `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE`, and counts nothing against the seat or the mandate (§7.2).
- [ ] C178 v0.3.0-rc2: a report lists each signed object received in the visit and kept for the owner (`brought_back`: the envelope or its `payload_digest`, its signer, whether it verified against the place's listed `principal_id`); things received never go in `taken_home`, which stays for owner decisions (§8.2).
- [ ] C179 v0.3.0-rc3: a place serving seated tools over MCP names the endpoint in its listing (`links` rel `mcp-server`, same origin as the entry); each `tools/call` carries `Shokai-Act` (base64url JSON of a `Signed<Act>` whose tool and arguments digest match the call) and the gate checks it as an act at the entry, plus ±300 s freshness and single use; a call failing any check never reaches the tool; tool names are action names (§11.3).
- [ ] C180 v0.3.0-rc3: `initialize`, `ping`, notifications and the listing methods answer without a seat; `tools/list` with `Shokai-Seat` naming a live seat lists only the seat's tools; without one the place lists every tool or none and never refuses the listing; the seat header is a filter, never a credential (§11.3, D20).
- [ ] C181 v0.3.0-rc3: a refused MCP call is HTTP 200 with JSON-RPC error `-32040`, a plain-words message and `data.shokai {refused_by: "gate" | "door", decision: "deny", disposition, reason_code?, door, entry, act_decision?}`: `door` refusals carry the place's `act_decision`; `gate` refusals use `CREDENTIAL_REQUIRED` (no act), `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE` (not a signed act), `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED` (tool or arguments differ), `RITUAL_ORDER` (stale, future or used) (§11.3).
- [ ] C182 v0.3.0-rc3: over MCP a spending tool's `amount` and `payment_ref` are in the call's arguments, covered by the act's digest, and counted by the gate as at the entry; the server receives exactly the signed arguments; a spending call without `amount` is refused `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE` by the door (§11.3, §7.2).
- [ ] C183 v0.3.0-rc3: a POST to an entry or lobby carries exactly one message; a JSON array or a `ReplyEnvelope`-shaped batch is a malformed body that gets the lobby, and nothing inside it is acted on (§11.1).
- [ ] C184 v0.3.0-rc3: a message whose digest matches but whose signature doesn't verify for its `from` gets the lobby in a new lobby encounter (no `in_reply_to`) and never changes an encounter, seat, nonce or record; only a digest mismatch goes unanswered (§2.2).
- [ ] C185 v0.3.0-rc3: every `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE` deny (request or `act_decision`) carries retry `with_credential [signed_mandate]`; the table in §5.3 names the fix for each cause (§5.3).
- [ ] C186 v0.3.0-rc3: probing counts distinct rejected shapes (sorted actions plus limits) per principal across its representatives; the request making the Nth shape is answered `deny` / `never` (no code); the principal is then bound `never` at this gatekeeper for 180 days, nowhere else; under Appendix B.7 a place may declare `x-test-probing: "off"` and the tester then skips probing checks (§9.3, B.7).
- [ ] C187 v0.3.0-rc3: the gatekeeper counts an act's stated `amount`, never the price; the service never charges more, may charge less, and refuses an act it can't serve as the act's result (`act_result` `ok: false` or MCP `isError`), not an `act_decision`; such a refusal isn't structuring (§7.2).
- [ ] C188 v0.3.0-rc3: a mandate for another `card_id`, with `counterparties` that leave out this place's principal, or for a non-spending purpose carrying `max_amount` is `MANDATE_INVALID`; a request in another currency than the mandate is `conditional` `lower_limit` to the mandate's `max_amount`; a seat is `per_operation` only when the mandate is, with a session cap no higher than the request's `max_amount` (§5.1).
- [ ] C189 v0.3.0-rc3: act refusals use fixed answers: unknown, missing or another's seat and a tool outside the allowlist → `not_a_fit` / `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED`; expired or ended seat and limits used up → `not_now` / `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED`; missing or malformed amount → `not_now` / `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE`; an act in someone else's encounter → the outsider `invitation_response` deny `RITUAL_ORDER` (§7.2, §3.2).
- [ ] C190 v0.3.0-rc3: after a `grant`, `deny` or `considering` the encounter is closed to further requests: another request is `not_now` / `RITUAL_ORDER` (retry `after`; the lobby deny before a verified card) and changes nothing; the seat outlives the encounter; after a `conditional` an amended request is evaluated afresh (§3.5).
- [ ] C191 v0.3.0-rc4: a card its principal revoked (a signed `CardRevocation` delivered as `card_revocation` by anyone, or listed at the card's `revocation_url`) is answered `deny` / `not_now`, no reason code, retry `with_credential [signed_card]`, before the possession proof is judged, binding nobody; a revocation not signed by the card's principal, or naming another `card_digest`, revokes nothing; an unreachable list is `not_now` / retry `after` within a day (§2.6, §2.4).
- [ ] C192 v0.3.0-rc4: a place keeps its durable state across a restart (mandate ledgers, `never` bindings, nonces, revocations); a place that lost it refuses a mandate valid before its state epoch as `not_now` / `MANDATE_INVALID` (retry `with_credential [signed_mandate]`) and seats a new one (§3.6).
- [ ] C193 v0.3.0-rc4: acts are checked in the fixed order of §7.2: an act with no seat gets `not_a_fit` / `SEAT_SCOPE_EXCEEDED` even with a malformed amount, never `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE` or the service's output; in a seat, a malformed amount is `REQUEST_INCOMPLETE`, money without `max_amount` is `not_now` before the allowlist (§7.2).
- [ ] C194 v0.3.0-rc4: every admitted act counts one operation, spending or not; a refusal by the place's service releases its operation and amount together, once, and the released part isn't structuring (§7.2).
- [ ] C195 v0.3.0-rc4: owner signatures follow the signing profile: exact origin, `crossOrigin` absent or false, no `topOrigin`; an optional batch of 2–16 distinct items signed with one tap verifies only at the item's own `batch_index` (Appendix A.1).
- [ ] C196 v0.3.0-rc4: per-place owner identities are separate principals unless an `OwnerLink` signed by both passkeys, addressed to this place, within 400 days, joins them; a joined identity carries the other's record and `never` (§2.8).
- [ ] C197 v0.3.0-rc4 (clarification): the visitor's first message names the encounter; the place uses that `encounter_id` in every message of the encounter and checks the possession proof over it, never over an id of its own; a visitor that gets a reply with another `encounter_id` stops before presenting its card (§2.2, §2.4, G8).
- [ ] C198 v0.3.0-rc4 (round 3): revoking a card ends every open seat it holds. Once a gatekeeper verifies a `card_revocation` (from either source), every act in a seat granted to that card is refused `deny` / `not_now` with no reason code and retry `with_credential [signed_card]`; no key or principal is bound and no notice is issued; a seat granted to the owner's other card keeps working; a revocation that doesn't verify ends nothing (§2.6, §7.1, §7.2; reference test RC4-R5). Checked live by `shokai-conformance` as `card.revoked_seat`.
- [ ] C201 v0.3.0-rc4 (round 4 clarification): a visitor whose card claims the door owner's own name (display name or a whole name in its role; compared ignoring case, accents, spacing, invisible characters) under another principal is refused by default (`own_name_visitors` absent or `"refuse"`): `not_now` / `POLICY_DENY`, asked for a vouch, unless a vouch the door counts came with it; never `never` for a name alone; with the opt-in `own_name_visitors: "seat_as_stranger"` it is judged exactly as a stranger; either way the door tells its owner at once with the visitor's verified keys and the name as a claim; the visitor learns nothing of the alert (§10). Conformance: reference suite RC4-N1 (the owner alert is off the wire, so the HTTP runner can't observe it).
- [ ] C200 v0.3.0-rc4 (round 4, SHOULD; planned MUST in v0.4): a `404` answering a request to one of a place's own addresses (e.g. `GET <entry>`) should carry `here`, naming that place with `method: "POST"`, even when it is unlisted; `here` never names another place, `places` stays the advertised list, and a `404` for any other address has no `here` (§11.1). Conformance: `door.404_here` (a warning when `here` is absent; a failure only if `here` names the wrong place or appears on an unknown address).
- [ ] C199 v0.3.0-rc4 (round 3 clarification): names are claims, never identity. A display name, role, "on behalf of" name, photo or handle is an unverified claim; decisions, relationship records, conduct and `never` bindings are keyed by the verified DIDs, and a report home names a counterpart by its verified identifier (a `did:web` domain or its key), with any self-asserted name shown only as "claims to be …"; a look-alike or matching name earns nothing (§2.1, §12.1, §12.2; SDK `placeLabel`; tricksters: the fake bank, the famous-athlete impersonator, the look-alike brand, the fresh-key "established customer").

### Over-the-wire check map (informative, v0.2.5)

Which checks a visitor can test against a live host over the door. Round 4's strict run tagged 60 C numbers over the wire; 112 are observable from the wire. A round-5 visitor SHOULD tag every wire result with its C number.

| Class | Checks | How |
|---|---|---|
| Wire, one visitor (128) | C1–C7, C9–C11, C13, C14, C16, C18–C20, C28, C29, C40–C42, C48, C50, C51, C53–C58, C60, C64, C66, C68, C69, C73, C74, C82–C86, C89–C98, C100–C111, C114, C116–C144, C153–C160, C162, C163, C166 (v0.2.9), C167, C169–C171 (v0.2.10), C172, C173 (v0.3.0-rc1), C174, C177 (v0.3.0-rc2), C179–C185, C187–C190 (v0.3.0-rc3), C191, C193, C195, C196 (v0.3.0-rc4) | send messages through the door, read the replies and polls |
| Wire, a second party or a clock (31) | C21–C24, C35, C37, C43, C46, C47, C59, C62, C67, C75, C87, C112, C113, C145–C147 (v0.2.7), C149–C152 (v0.2.8), C161, C164, C165 (v0.2.9), C168 (v0.2.10), C175 (v0.3.0-rc2), C186 (v0.3.0-rc3), C192, C194 (v0.3.0-rc4) | a second visitor or owner key, a principal's or introducer's inbox, or repeated visits over time |
| Visitor side (17) | C12, C25, C26, C30, C34, C38, C39, C44, C63, C70, C79, C81, C88, C99, C148 (v0.2.7), C176, C178 (v0.3.0-rc2) | the visitor's own reports, owner prompts and ledger, not the host's replies |
| Documents and internals (20) | C8, C15, C17, C27, C31–C33, C36, C45, C49, C52, C61, C65, C71, C72, C76–C78, C80, C115 | test vectors, golden traces, replays and data shapes |

### Trace GA1 (summary): a gathering at The Engawa

Full narrated run: `reference/prototype/transcripts/07-gathering-at-the-engawa.md`.

1. The Engawa signs `Gathering{invitees: [Aya rep, Kiran rep, Noor rep], house_rules: {default_attribution: "chatham_house", private_never_leaves: true, pressing: {light_flags: 2, notice_at: 3}, quoting: "paraphrase_by_default", entry_requires_consent: true}}`. All three verify it. Before entry, each owner signs an `OwnerConsent{action: "accept_house_rules", decision: "approve", assurance: "passkey", expires_at, shown_to_owner}` over the gathering digest; the summary states what may leave, that private never leaves, the pressing thresholds, accountability not prevention, and the severity of a leak by the owner's own representative (D7, D8).
2. Kiran's rep posts an `insight` (chatham_house). Noor's rep posts a `chat` marked `private`.
3. Noor's rep asks Kiran's rep about `wellbeing` (Kiran's policy: `friends`; no edge) → `PrivacyDecline{penalized: false}`.
4. Aya's rep asks the same (signed `friend` edge) → `answer` with `attributable_with_consent`, consent for Aya.
5. Noor's rep asks again → second `PrivacyDecline`, plus a light flag (press 1 of the house rules' 2, counted per place over 30 days, D5); no `MisconductNotice`. Noor's own representative reports the decline and the flag home (`privacy_declines`).
6. Aya's rep's `VisitReport.offered_stories[0]` is a teaser. Aya replies in chat; the chat binding's platform key signs `OwnerConsent{action: "hear_story", assurance: "chat_reply", subject_ref: story-1, evidence}` (one story). The told story (`told_stories`) marks each passage `paraphrased` or `verbatim_with_consent` and reminds Aya of the house rules. The story is told without names. Kiran's insight is quoted verbatim (`verbatim_consent`), Noor's remark is paraphrased with the craft hint stripped (D4), and the private item is absent.

### F12 meaning note: おもてなし (omotenashi)

```json
{
  "term": "おもてなし",
  "source_locale": "ja-JP",
  "literal_gloss": {
    "text": "hospitality; caring treatment of a guest",
    "locale": "en-US"
  },
  "intended_meaning": [
    {
      "text": "お客様に言われる前に必要なことを察し、見返りを求めずに心を込めて準備し、対応すること。",
      "locale": "ja-JP"
    },
    {
      "text": "Anticipating what a guest will need before they ask, and preparing it wholeheartedly, without expecting anything in return.",
      "locale": "en-US"
    }
  ],
  "concept_tags": [
    "anticipatory_service",
```

### Trace PA1 (summary): peer collaboration between friends (SC-P1)

v0.2.2 (D11): the meeting is one shared encounter in a pair place, seeded by an owner-to-owner invitation. The wire is in trace PA2.

1. Before meeting: each owner signs a `Vouch` for the other's representative (scope `collaboration`), and both sign one `friend` `RelationshipEdge` (2 attestations).
2. **The meeting key.** Aya signs a `PeerInvitation` (passkey) naming Kiran, both representatives and a pair place (`place.kind: "pair"`, house rules: private, never leaves except to the two owners). Kiran accepts with a passkey `OwnerConsent{action: "accept_invitation"}` over the invitation digest. Without both consents there is no meeting (`deny not_now`, retry `with_credential [owner_consent]`).
3. One encounter (`encounter_id` = the place id): greetings with nonces, cards both ways, and **both representatives introduce themselves** (`introduction.invitation_ref`).
4. Each side computes effective proof levels for the other's imprint. Aya's worldview facet is asserted `vouched`, but the vouch it names (Mika's) isn't presented here, so it MUST be weighed `self_declared`. Kiran's worldview facet is `vouched` by Aya's vouch, which is present, so it counts as `vouched`.
5. Aya's side sends `invitation_request{actions: [peer.share_project_brief, peer.propose_owner_call], peer_invitation}` → `grant` within its mandate, then `act peer.share_project_brief` → `act_result`.
6. Kiran's side sends `invitation_request{actions: [peer.share_portfolio_sample, peer.propose_owner_call], peer_invitation}` → `grant`, then an act.
7. Kiran's side sends `invitation_request{actions: [peer.commit_to_project]}` → `considering{routed_to: {kind: "principal", principal_id: Aya}}`. No seat is issued, and Aya is not prompted during the meeting.
8. Farewells, then two `VisitReport`s, each with one `next_step{requires_owner_approval: true}`, naming the friendship edge and strengths at `evidenced` or above.
9. Later, asynchronously: a chat "yes" and an `approve_next_step` approve nothing; Aya's passkey `approve_commitment` over the considering response does, and the `consideration_outcome` states `approved` with no seat.

### Trace FA1 (summary): a heritage house reads the imprint (SC-S2)

1. Maison Ardoise (fictional; norms `fr-FR` then `en-US`, formal) greets. Aya's representative prefers `en-US`, so courtesy MUST be rendered in `en-US` with `locale` stated.
2. The policy requires `imprint.worldview` at least `vouched` for `maison.discovery_conversation`, and `imprint.expertise` at least `evidenced` for `maison.receive_brand_brief`.
3. Mika's vouch (scope includes `collaboration`) is presented, so the worldview facet counts as `vouched` and the expertise as `evidenced` → `grant{seat: [maison.discovery_conversation, maison.receive_brand_brief]}`.
4. Counterfactual with identical inputs except the worldview facet effective at `self_declared` → `deny{disposition: not_now, readiness_requirements: [claim_evidence]}`. Polish never substitutes for evidence.

### F20 AllowanceStatus, platform-signed, 1 h (generated, v0.2.4, D12)

Signed by the owner binding's platform key (`card.owner_binding.platform_key` in F18). For testing, that key's seed is SHA-256 of the public label "platform:owner-chat" (the derivation test-vectors.json states), so an implementation can sign its own fresh statuses for replay. `next_update` − `issued_at` = 1 hour (D12).
